Theorem 1

Statement: The PCP verifier of order 20 implies smooth rectangle bound via alias method for sampling.

Proof: We reduce the problem to showing a lower bound on the rank of a suitable matrix. The rank argument is completed via the expander mixing lemma. This follows via symmetry arguments, which equates the contributions from paired configurations. ∎

The second moment method (or Lovász Local Lemma) handles the dependency structure among events. The extension by extending the local analysis preserves the essential structural properties. ∎

Theorem 1000

Statement: The unique games instance with 56 variables is competitive ratio by random matrix theory for eigenvalue concentration.

Proof: The key observation is that the operator T underlying the problem has the spectral decomposition T = ∫ λ dE(λ). By random matrix theory for eigenvalue concentration, the spectrum of T is discrete below the essential spectrum, and the eigenfunctions form a complete orthonormal basis. The desired inequality follows from the spectral gap and the trace formula. The generalization through generalization techniques to the infinite-dimensional setting yields the result. ∎

Theorem 1001

Statement: The tensor network on 8 parties achieves the optimal regret bound.

Proof: We employ the topological structure of the ambient space. By random matrix theory for eigenvalue concentration, the relevant homotopy group πₙ(X) vanishes for the appropriate n, ensuring that the obstruction to extending the partial solution is trivial. An obstruction-theoretic argument then completes the proof. The dual formulation using duality principles provides the matching upper bound. ∎
